Replace the value of reserved codepoints to (1/2)15/4.<br>- Phase: uniform distribution, , for | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601254](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601254.zip) |
| Conclusion | There is no consensus on determining the actual payload size of the inference report based on UE indications other than RI. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601254](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601254.zip) |
| Conclusion | Configuration of pairing ID in the CSI report for inference is sufficient to distinguish the CSI feedback with two-sided model with other legacy CSI reports. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601254](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601254.zip) |
| Conclusion | There is no RAN1 consensus to support periodic TRS triggered by a MAC-CE in MSG4 when UE transition from IDLE/INACTIVE to CONNECTED mode. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600647](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600647.zip) |
| Agreement | Support a MAC-CE in MSG4 PDSCH for triggering aperiodic SRS-AS/CSI-RS indicates at least<br>- The slot offset used to determine the transmission slot of aperiodic SRS-AS if triggered,<br>- The slot offset used to determine the transmission slot of aperiodic CSI-RS associated with AP CSI reporting if triggered, and<br>- The slot offset used to determine the transmission slot of aperiodic CSI-RS of the first TRS burst, if triggered.<br>- The slot offset used to determine the transmission slot of the PUSCH for aperiodic CSI reporting, if triggered, via the UL grant provided in the MAC-CE<br>  - FFS: How to indicate the slot offset by the UL grant provided in the MAC-CE<br>Note: Whether to indicate the other settings (i.e., comb offset, repetition factor, cyclic shift and the number of TRS bursts, the parameters (e.g., slot offset, time gap) to determine the Tx slot(s) of the TRS burst(s), if triggered, other than the first TRS burst) via MAC-CE in MSG4 are separately discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600647](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600647.zip) |
| Agreement | For aperiodic CSI reporting for a SCell(s), triggered based on a legacy SCell activation command activating the SCell(s), the SCell activation command indicates a UL grant for a PUSCH to carry the aperiodic CSI report(s) for the CSI report configuration(s) triggered for the SCell(s)<br>- Strive to reuse legacy UL grant design in RAR<br>- The aperiodic CSI report(s) is reported in the same PUSCH.<br>- FFS: The serving cell where the PUSCH is scheduled in | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600647](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600647.zip) |
| Agreement | To determine the transmission/reception slot of the DL/UL signalling triggered/scheduled based on a legacy SCell activation command (i.e., aperiodic SRS-AS, aperiodic CSI-RS and the PUSCH scheduled for aperiodic CSI reporting), for applying a slot offset between a reference slot and a transmission/reception slot of the DL/UL signalling triggered/scheduled based on the legacy SCell activation command, the reference slot is the first slot that is 3ms after UE transmits HARQ-ACK for the PDSCH carrying the SCell activation command | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600647](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600647.zip) |
| Agreement | To determine the transmission/reception slot of a MSG4-triggered DL/UL signalling (i.e., aperiodic SRS-AS, aperiodic TRS, aperiodic CSI-RS and the PUSCH scheduled for aperiodic CSI reporting), for applying a slot offset between a reference slot and a transmission/reception slot of the MSG4-triggered DL/UL signalling, the reference slot is the first slot that is 3ms after UE transmits HARQ-ACK for the MSG4 PDSCH carrying the MAC-CE. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600647](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600647.zip) |
| Agreement | For SCell transition from deactivation to activation, at least support triggering early aperiodic SRS and/or early aperiodic CSI reporting based on Rel-17 fast SCell activation MAC-CE.<br>- FFS: Support of triggering early aperiodic SRS and/or early aperiodic CSI reporting based on Rel-15 SCell activation MAC-CE | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600648](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600648.zip) |
| Agreement | For CSI-RS frequency-domain densities ρ = 1/3 and 1/6, support new CSI subband sizes as follows:<br>\| Bandwidth part size (PRBs) \| CSI subband size (PRBs) \|<br>\| 24-72 \| 6 (FFS: 12) \|<br>\| 73-144 \| 12 (FFS: 6) \|<br>\| 145-275 \| 24 (FFS: 30) \|<br>Note: Legacy PRG sizes are reused without enhancement. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600648](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600648.zip) |
| Conclusion | No enhancement to the following legacy specification restriction on frequency density of CSI-RS port in a CSI subband for the frequency-domain densities ρ = 1/3 and 1/6, i.e., the following from Clause 5.2.1.4 in TS 38.214 shall be applied:<br>- A UE is not expected to be configured with csi-ReportingBand which contains a subband where a CSI-RS resource linked to the CSI Report setting has the frequency density of each CSI-RS port per PRB in the subband less than the configured density of the CSI-RS resource. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600648](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600648.zip) |
| Agreement | MSG4-triggered aperiodic TRS is QCLed with the SSB the UE identified during the initial access procedure, on QCL-Type C.<br>- FSS:  Whether to consider QCL Type-D when applicable. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600648](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600648.zip) |
| Agreement | On triggering mechanism for early SRS/CSI-RS/CSI, via a DCI indicating switching out of dormancy for an SCell(s) based on legacy UE-specific DCI formats (e.g., DCI format 0_1/0_3/1_1/1_3),<br>- For triggering aperiodic SRS-AS, the SRS resource set(s) triggered for the SCell(s) is determined according to ‘SRS request’ field in the legacy DCI format 0_1/0_3/1_1/1_3 indicating switching out of dormancy for the SCell(s)<br>  - FFS: How to determine the serving cell(s) triggered with aperiodic SRS<br>  - FFS: How to coexist with legacy triggering mechanism for aperiodic SRS<br>- For triggering aperiodic CSI reporting and associated CSI-RS for CSI, the CSI report configuration(s) triggered for the SCell(s) is determined according to ‘CSI request’ field in the DCI formats 0_1/0_3 indicating switching out of dormancy for the SCell(s)<br>  - Note: UE determines the serving cell(s) which the associated CSI-RS is triggered for, via carrier configured in the CSI report configuration(s), where the CSI report configuration(s) is determined according to the ‘CSI request’ field.<br>  - Note: No additional RAN1 spec enhancement is needed to support aperiodic CSI reporting<br>Note: Legacy DCI format(s) for switching out of SCell dormancy is reused without introducing new DCI field or resizing the existing DCI field. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600649](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600649.zip) |
| Agreement | On how to determine the serving cell(s) triggered with aperiodic SRS via ‘SRS request’ field in a DCI indicating switching out of dormancy based on legacy DCI formats 0_1/0_3/1_1/1_3, study the following alternatives:<br>- Alt-1: The SRS trigger state indicated in ‘SRS request’ field applies to,<br>  - The cell(s) scheduled by legacy mechanism and configured with aperiodic SRS resource set(s) associated with the SRS trigger state; and<br>  - The SCell(s) which is indicated with switching out of dormancy and configured with aperiodic SRS resource set(s) for SRS-AS associated with the SRS trigger state.<br>- Alt-2: For DCI format 0_1/1_1, support “Carrier Indicator” field in the DCI to indicate the serving cell(s) triggered with aperiodic SRS, if the “Carrier Indicator” field indicates non-zero value.<br>Note: The other alternative(s) is not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600649](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600649.zip) |

| Agreement | Study TCI/QCL-related aspects, e.g., definition of QCL/TCI-state, QCL property/chain<br>On beam management for DL and UL of 6GR, at least the following aspects should be studied:<br>- Beam measurement(prediction)/report/indication within a same TRP, i.e., single-TRP, in a cell/carrier;<br>- Beam measurement(prediction)/report/indication among different TRPs, i.e., multi-TRP, in a cell/carrier;<br>- Beam measurement(prediction)/report/indication among different cells/carrier, i.e., inter-cell/carrier<br>- Note: Both AI/ML and non-AI/ML related mechanism(s) for the above can be further studied.<br>Note-1: Which multi-TRP transmission scheme for study will be discussed under other agenda. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Study of UE-initiated/event-driven beam management (UEIBM) mechanisms for 6GR, covering at least the following aspects:<br>- Event definition and the corresponding target use case.<br>- UL transmission, UE’s behavior, and procedure for a triggered event, including the necessity and design of the associated network’s response.<br>- Note 1: Both AI/ML and non-AI/ML related mechanism(s) for the above can be further studied.<br>- Note 2: UE-initiated/event-driven CSI reporting is not discussed in this agenda. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |

| Agreement | - From RAN1 perspective, on measurement quantization of Level C/D for position/velocity measurement, quantization methods in existing specifications, e.g., TS 23.032, TS 38.455, TS 38.355, TS 37.355 can be considered.<br>- From RAN1 perspective, on measurement quantization of Level C for delay/angle measurement, quantization methods in existing specifications, e.g., TS 38.133 can be considered.<br>  - For Doppler measurement, same number of quantized bits as radial velocity can be considered.<br>- Note: above does not take into account the potential impact from measurement quantization on the sensing performance. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601381](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601381.zip) |

| Agreement | The following Text Proposal to Conclusion section in TR 38.765 is endorsed.<br>\| The performance for UAV sensing use case with gNB monostatic sensing is evaluated based on the evaluation assumptions in Annex A (including UMa-AV, Sensing Tx/Rx operating simultaneously, FR1), with detailed assumptions and modelling reported by the companies as captured in Annex B. Among all the reported evaluation results as captured in Annex B, Baseline 1 (high BS TX power 52dBm with 80dB antenna isolation) By utilizing measurements from multiple or all TRPs, results from 9/10 sources achieve the performance objectives. By utilizing measurements from single TRP, results from 3/11 sources achieve the performance objectives. Baseline 2 (low BS TX power 37dBm with 65dB antenna isolation) By utilizing measurements from multiple or all TRPs, results from 7/9 sources achieve the performance objectives. By utilizing measurements from single TRP, results from 2/9 sources achieve the performance objectives \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601382](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601382.zip) |
| Conclusion | - From RAN1 perspective, the issue on ambiguity handling in delay/range, Doppler/radial velocity, angle, position and 3D velocity measurements is identified, but not further discussed in Rel-20 NR ISAC.<br>  - Note: it does not prevent further discussion in future.<br>  - Note: this issue may or may not be addressed by NW implementation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601382](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601382.zip) |
| Agreement | - The velocity measurement of Option D1/D4 is 3D velocity, optional radial velocity.<br>  - Note: in the deployment where 3D velocity cannot be reported, Level D should not be reported. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601382](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601382.zip) |
| Agreement | - The velocity measurement of Option C2/C5 is radial velocity. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601382](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601382.zip) |
| Conclusion | - No further discussion on the assistance information from SF to RAN is expected in Rel-20 NR ISAC from RAN1 study perspective. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601382](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601382.zip) |
| Agreement | - Confirm the definition of measurement Level B by removing the square brackets<br>- Level B: Amplitude and phase profile of delay, and/or Doppler, and/or angle per TRP for a given time stamp by using window(s) of the [consecutive] samples in at least one profile of delay, and/or Doppler and/or angle domain | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601382](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601382.zip) |
| Agreement | - From RAN1 perspective, the following methods can be considered to define the quantization for the measurements of Level A/B,<br>  - e.g., floating point, uniform scalar, non-uniform scalar<br>  - other quantization method is not precluded.<br>- Note: above doesn’t take into account the potential impact from the measurement quantization on sensing performance<br>- Note: No more discussion on measurement quantization on Level A/B is expected in the study item from RAN1 perspective | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601383](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601383.zip) |
| Agreement | - From RAN1 perspective, the metric related to power can be considered for Level C measurement report.<br>- From RAN1 perspective, the metric related to power (if applicable) can be considered for Level D measurement report.<br>- Note: metric related to power may be different from those defined for communication and between Level C and Level D<br>- From RAN1 perspective, confidence metrics for the measurements can be considered for Level C/D measurement report<br>- No more discussion on the metric related to power and confidence metrics is expected in the study item from RAN1 study perspective | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601383](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601383.zip) |
| Agreement | - For Level C, at least the following examples of assistance information are identified as needed to assist the further processing at Sensing Function. The assistance information may or may not be provided by RAN.<br>  - Location of TRP if at least delay/range, Doppler/radial velocity or angle is reported<br>  - If a measurement is reported in LCS, LCS to GCS transformation information<br>- For level D, if radial velocity is not reported, no assistance information is identified from RAN1 perspective.<br>- No more discussion on the assistance information on Level C/D from RAN to SF is expected in the study item from RAN1 perspective | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601383](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601383.zip) |
| Agreement | - From RAN1 perspective, on measurement quantization of Level C/D for confidence metric, the existing specifications, e.g., TS 23.032, TS 38.455, TS 38.355, TS 37.355 can be considered.<br>- Note: above does not take into account the potential impact from measurement quantization on the sensing performance.<br>- Note: No more discussion on measurement quantization on Level C/D for the metric related to power and confidence metric is expected in the study item from RAN1 perspective | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601384](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601384.zip) |
| Agreement | - For Level A/B, at least the following examples of assistance information are identified as needed to assist the further processing at Sensing Function. The assistance information may or may not be provided by RAN.<br>  - Level A<br>    - Location of TRP<br>    - Step size in delay domain if samples in delay domain is reported<br>    - Time information related to OFDM symbols used for measurement<br>    - Antenna configuration and/or beam information<br>  - Level B<br>    - Location of TRP<br>    - LCS to GCS transformation information<br>    - Information related to sampling of delay, doppler and/or angle profile<br>- Additional assistance information is not precluded<br>- No more discussion on the assistance information on Level A/B from RAN to SF is expected in the study item from RAN1 perspective | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601384](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601384.zip) |
| Agreement | The payload size estimation of each measurement level/option for NR ISAC can be done by the following formulas<br>- Level A: Msubcarrier* Ncpi *Mtxport*Nrxport*Ntrp*(Nquan1+ Nquan2)<br>Where,<br>  - Msubcarrier: number of subcarriers in a OFDM symbols<br>  - Ncpi: number of OFDM symbols within CPI<br>  - Mtxport: number of reference signal antenna port for sensing purpose<br>  - Nrxport: number of Rx antenna ports<br>  - Ntrp: number of TRPs for payload size calculation<br>  - Nquan1: number of quantized bits for amplitude<br>  - Nquan2: number of quantized bits for angle<br>- Level B:<br>  - Delay-Doppler Profile: Mdelay *Ndoppler *Mtxport* Nrxport *Ntrp *(Nquan1+ Nquan2)<br>  - Delay-angle Profile: Mdelay * Ncpi *Mtxport*Nangle *Ntrp *(Nquan1+ Nquan2)<br>  - Delay-Doppler-angle Profile: Mdelay *Ndoppler *Mtxport*Nangle *Ntrp *(Nquan1+ Nquan2)<br>  - Delay Profile: Mdelay *Ncpi*Mtxport*Nrxport *Ntrp *(Nquan1+ Nquan2)<br>Where,<br>  - Mdelay: number of delay samples in the window<br>  - Ndoppler: number of Doppler samples in the window<br>  - Nangle: number of angle samples in the window<br>- Level C:<br>  - C1/C2/C3: Np1*Ntrp *<br>  - C4/C5: Np2*Ntrp *<br>Where,<br>  - Np1 is total number of paths<br>  - Np2 is total number of points<br>  - Nparam is 5 including e.g.,<br>    - C1/C3/C4: delay, Doppler, horizontal angle, vertical angle, power measurement<br>    - C2/C5: position (x, y, z), radial velocity, power measurement<br>  - Nquan,i: number of quantized bits<br>  - Ntrp: number of TRPs for payload size calculation<br>- Level D:<br>  - Nobject*Ngnb * for options D1/2<br>  - Mpoints* Ngnb * for options D3/4<br>Where,<br>  - Nobject is total number of targets<br>  - Mpoints is total number of scattering points of all targets<br>  - Ngnb is fixed to 1 | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601384](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601384.zip) |
| Agreement | - The following assumptions on the parameters can be used to estimate payload size of different Levels/Options of measurements for NR ISAC for calculation purpose<br>\| Parameters \| Assumptions \| Relation to maximum payload calculation \|<br>\| Nsubcarriers \| 3276 for 100MHz, 30kHz SCS \|  \|<br>\| Ncpi \| Min 64, medium 160, max 320 symbols per CPI \|  \|<br>\| Mtxport \| 1, medium 4, max 9 \| Note: value obtained based on evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| Nrxport \| 64 \|  \|<br>\| Ntrp \| 3 \|  \|<br>\| Ngnb \| 1 \|  \|<br>\| Nquan1, i.e., amplitude \| Min 16, medium 16, max 32 \|  \|<br>\| Nquan2, i.e., phase \| Min 16, medium 16, max 32 \|  \|<br>\|  \| Min 16, medium 16, max 32 \|  \|<br>\| Mdelay \| Min 288 (CP length, UMa) Medium/max: 467 for 2*570m (UMa) Max: 1436 for 2*1732m (RMa) \|  \|<br>\| Ndoppler \| same as Ncpi \|  \|<br>\| Nangle \| B2/B3: 32, 360 \| Note: value obtained based on evaluation assumptions Note: 32 assumes non-uniform sampling Note: 360 assumes uniform oversampling \|<br>\| Np \| 30, 60, 120 \| to calculate min/medium/max for Level-C Note: value obtained based on evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| Nobject for D1/D2 \| 15 \| Note: value obtained based on evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| Mpoints for D3/D4 \| 20 \| Note: value obtained based on evaluation assumptions \|<br>  - For Level C<br>    - Nparam=5 parameters for measurements<br>  - For Level D<br>    - Nparam=7 parameters for measurements<br>    - for D2/D3/D4, one more parameter of target ID is assumed<br>Note: for B2/B3, minimum value and maximum value can be different between non-uniform sampling case and uniform oversampling case<br>Note: RAN1 does not require all the parameters to take the minimum values at the same time or the maximum values at the same time<br>Note: above does not take into account the potential impact from measurement quantization on the sensing performance.<br>Note: above does not take into account the potential impact of different Levels/Options on the sensing performance. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601384](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601384.zip) |
| Agreement | The following Text Proposal to Conclusion section in TR 38.765 is endorsed.<br>\| The study in physical layer has identified a number of Levels/Options for measurement report as described in Clause 5. Different Levels/Options for measurement report are associated with different payload overhead, assistance information and other higher layer aspects. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601384](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601384.zip) |
| Agreement | Draft TR R1-2601711 is endorsed in principle. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-ISAC]` |

| Agreement | Regarding FTP3 extension with multiple packet sizes:<br>- The number of packet sizes X = 2;<br>- For each packet size S_i, the packets arrive according to Poisson distribution (as FTP 3) with mean inter-arrival time T_i  (or arrival rate λ_i where T_i = 1/ λ_i);<br>- For Alt1: Y=1 packet size is simulated for each UE<br>  - For FTP3-extension with X=2, K is the ratio between arrival rates of the packet sizes, i.e., λ_1= K·λ_2, with K>=1, assuming S_1<S_2,<br>  - G>=1 is the ratio between the number of UEs with packet size S_1 and S_2, respectively.<br>  - Values of S_i, λ_i, i=1, 2, and G can be decided in evaluation phase.<br>  - Note: The following table is an illustration of the traffic configurations.<br>\|  \| Example 1 \| Example 1 \| Example 2 \| Example 2 \| Example 3 \| Example 3 \|<br>\| Packet Type \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \|<br>\| S_i \| 10KB \| 500KB \| 4KB \| 400KB \| 10KB \| 100KB \|<br>\| λ_i \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \|<br>\| G \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \|<br>\| G \| N_1 is the number of UEs with small packet size S_1; N_2 is the number of UEs with large packet size S_2. \| N_1 is the number of UEs with small packet size S_1; N_2 is the number of UEs with large packet size S_2. \| N_1 is the number of UEs with small packet size S_1; N_2 is the number of UEs with large packet size S_2. \| N_1 is the number of UEs with small packet size S_1; N_2 is the number of UEs with large packet size S_2. \| N_1 is the number of UEs with small packet size S_1; N_2 is the number of UEs with large packet size S_2. \| N_1 is the number of UEs with small packet size S_1; N_2 is the number of UEs with large packet size S_2. \|<br>- For Alt2: Y=X=2 packet sizes are simulated for each UE<br>  - The packet of each size is generated following the independent Poisson Process (S_i, λ_i) with i=1,2.<br>  - K is the ratio between arrival rates of the packet sizes, i.e., λ_1= K·λ_2, with K>=1, assuming S_1<S_2.<br>  - Values of (S_i, λ_i) with i=1,2 can be decided in evaluation phase.<br>  - Note: The following table is an illustration of the traffic configurations.<br>\|  \| Example 1 \| Example 1 \| Example 2 \| Example 2 \| Example 3 \| Example 3 \|<br>\| Packet Type \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \|<br>\| S_i \| [4]KB \| [800]KB \| 100KB \| 500KB \| 10KB \| 100KB \|<br>\| λ_i \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |

| Agreement | Following metrics are used for SLS evaluations for multi-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM studies.<br>- User perceived throughput (UPT), including:<br>  - High percentile (90%)<br>  - mean<br>  - median<br>  - cell edge (5 & 10-percentile)<br>- Optional for full buffer traffic only: cell average throughput<br>- Companies are encouraged to report the CDF of instantaneous UL TX power across all UEs<br>- Companies are encouraged to report the statistics on the UL TX rank.<br>- Companies are encouraged to report the statistics on the applied MCS. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600788](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600788.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| Agreement | Following metrics are used for LLS evaluation for multi-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM studies.<br>- BLER curves (for a subset of NR MCS (covering whole range with spanning), HARQ re-transmissions disabled) for same transmission rank, for same resource allocation and same transmission power for DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM<br>  - Companies may derive Link-level throughput vs SNR based on BLER curves<br>- Netgain | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600788](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600788.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| Agreement | For 2-layer/2Tx UL DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM studies, following two cases are considered.<br>- UE capable of only non-coherent precoder<br>  - For UL CP-OFDM and DFT-s-OFDM rank-2 transmission, only non-coherent precoder option is allowed.<br>- UE capable of fully-coherent precoder<br>  - For UL CP-OFDM rank-2 transmission, all NR precoder options are allowed.<br>  - For UL DFT-s-OFDM rank-2 transmission, only non-coherent precoder option is allowed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600788](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600788.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| Agreement | For the multi-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM studies, the NR reference should be evaluated assuming the Release 16 full power mode 1 to be enabled. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600788](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600788.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| Agreement | The following UL low-PAPR proposals for DFT-s-OFDM are for further consideration:<br>- Non-AI-ML-based<br>  - FDSS (R1-2601092, R1-2600751, R1-2600801, R1-2600823, R1-2600914, R1-2601156)<br>  - FDSS – spectrum extension (R1-2600027, R1-2601092, R1-2600823, R1-2600751, R1-2600914, R1-2601156, R1-2600261)<br>  - FDSS – spectrum truncation for π/2 BPSK (R1-2601268, R1-2601212, R1-2601092, R1-2601156)<br>  - Tone Reservation (R1-2600261, R1-2600716, R1-2601268)<br>    - For π/2 BPSK or other modulation orders<br>  - GMSK-Approximation based FDSS (R1-2600823)<br>  - 3-tap filter based FDSS (R1-2508684)<br>  - CFR-SE (R1-2600499)<br>  - Offset-QAM or π/2-PAM with FDSS-spectrum truncation (R1-2600909, R1-2600138, R1-2601268, R1-2600751)<br>  - Offset-QAM or π/2-PAM with FDSS-spectrum extension (R1-2600909, R1-2600138, R1-2600751, R1-2600823)<br>  - Interpolation-based modulation (R1-2600261)<br>  - AFDM based on DFT-s-OFDM (R1-2600999, R1-2601019)<br>- AI-ML-based<br>  - AI/ML-based waveform (R1-2600499, R1-2600751)<br>Note: tdoc numbers described in each proposal provide information on the proposal for further consideration, and it does not mean these tdocs support the proposal. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600789](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600789.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |

S: 16 \|<br>\| Decoding algorithm for Polar code \| CRC-aided SCL decoding Companies to report the list sizes either 8 or 16. \|<br>\| Target BLER of UCI payload \| 10-2, 10-3 \|<br>Note: 11 bits CRC per segment, rate matching, channel interleaver and code-block concatenation follow NR.<br>Note: Companies to report other values that are used in their evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601568](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601568.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| Agreement | For the study of LDPC extension beyond NR range, the maximum lifting size is not larger than 384<br>- FFS: the exact value of the maximum lifting size | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601569](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601569.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| Agreement | For the base graph of LDPC extension beyond NR range, the following is identified in RAN1#124 for further study<br>- The LDPC extension is quasi-cyclic LDPC codes, the parity check matrix of quasi-cyclic LDPC Codes is defined at least by a matrix H of size (mb×z)×(nb×z), which consists of sub-block matrices of size z×z, where each sub-block matrix is composed by circularly shifted matrices or zero matrices. Wherein, mb, nb and z are integers larger than 1.<br>- The values of mb, nb and z are FFS.<br>- The row weight of circularly shifted matrices is less than or equal to 2.<br>- FFS: Whether row weight of all circularly shifted matrices is 1, or the row weight of some circularly shifted matrices is 1 and the row weight of some circularly shifted matrices is 2. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601666](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601666.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| Agreement | For study of LDPC extension beyond NR range, at least the following metrics are considered for the performance comparison among BG1 and BG(s)/PCM(s) proposed by companies<br>- Computational complexity difference to achieve the same BLER performance as BG1 under the reference maximum number of iterations of BG1.<br>- SNR performance difference with the same computation complexity as BG1 under the reference maximum number of iterations of BG1.<br>- The reference maximum number of iterations is 5, 7, 10, 15 based on BG1.<br>  - Other values can be also reported by companies<br>- Note: Companies can report the average number of iterations difference to achieve the same performance as BG1 under the reference maximum number of iterations of BG1. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601666](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601666.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| Agreement | - Parameters affect the PS complexity/storage/latency and BLER and throughput performance trade-off are at least:<br>- DM output length in unit of I/Q symbol,<br>- # of bits shaped per I/Q,<br>- # of shaping parameters<br>- # of DM blocks needed to support the target throughput of 6GR<br>- DM algorithm and bit-width of variables in the DM algorithm<br>- Impact of mismatch on the quantization bitwidth between DM and DDM.<br>- Parameters affect the GS complexity/storage/latency and BLER and throughput performance trade-off are at least:<br>- 1D-NUC or 2D-NUC<br>- Constellations and  # of constellations<br>- Bitwidth for describing the constellation<br>- Bits to constellation mapping<br>- The values of these parameters used in evaluations shall be submitted together with performance results.<br>- Companies are encouraged to provide evaluations for different combination parameters to study different performance and complexity/storage/latency trade-offs to provide proper assessment including feasibility . | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Agreement | For the study of introducing DL 4K uniform QAM and UL 1K uniform QAM, focus on the following use cases:<br>- FWA deployed outdoor<br>- FWA deployed indoor<br>- FFS: In-door hot-spot.<br>- For SLS of DL 4K uniform QAM and UL 1K uniform QAM for CPE under FWA scenarios, assume the following:<br>- For layout<br>  - Dense Urban<br>  - UMA<br>- FFS: Other SLS parameters | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601550](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601550.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

| Agreement | - For PS, potential impact to the TX/RX chain functionality blocks are identified as follows:<br>- TX chain<br>  - (Modified) TBS calculation<br>  - (Modified) CB segmentation<br>  - (New) DM functionalities<br>    - Bit splitting: Split to shaped bits and unshaped bits<br>    - DM<br>    - Bit concatenation/multiplexing: Concatenate/multiplex DM output and unshaped bits<br>  - (Modified) Bit interleaver and Bit selection<br>  - (Modified) Scrambling: shaped bits should not be scrambled to keep the target distribution<br>  - (Modified) Modulation: Power normalization needed for shaped constellation<br>- RX chain<br>  - (Modified) TBS calculation<br>  - (New) DM functionalities<br>    - Bit splitting: Split to shaped bits and unshaped bits<br>    - DDM<br>    - Bit concatenation/demultiplexing: Concatenate/demultiplex DDM output with unshaped bits<br>  - (Modified) Bit de-interleaver and Bit selection<br>  - (Modified) Descrambling:<br>  - (Modified) Demodulation: Prior probability used in demodulation<br>  - (Modified) CB concatenation<br>Companies are encouraged to provide design details for the modification needed for above functionalities.<br>Companies are encouraged to explain the reason if a functionality block is not impacted. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601551](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601551.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Agreement | The draft LS in R1-2601663 is endorsed. The final LS in R1-2601664 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601662](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601662.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Agreement | - For GS, potential impact to the TX/RX chain functionality blocks compared to NR are identified as follows:<br>- TX chain<br>  - Mapper<br>    - Bit to constellation symbol mapping<br>  - Modulation symbol generation<br>- RX chain<br>  - Demodulation of received symbols<br>  - Demapper<br>Companies are encouraged to identify potential impact on TX, RX chains if both NUC and uniform QAM are supported.<br>Companies are encouraged to provide design details for the modification needed for above functionalities.<br>Companies are encouraged to explain the reason if a functionality block is not impacted. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601662](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601662.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

| Agreement | 6GR shall at least be capable of configuring the same TDD slot configurations as TDD slot configurations deployed in 5G NR. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | Study link direction determination for dynamic TDD, considering at least<br>- UE PDCCH monitoring efforts and power consumption<br>- Signaling overhead<br>- Scheduling flexibility<br>- Avoid duplicated functionalities<br>- Collision handling | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| Agreement | For initial access and mobility in 6GR, study the following deployment scenarios<br>- Single beam and multi-beam based deployments<br>- Single TRP and multi-TRP based deployments<br>- Single carrier and multi-carrier deployments<br>- Other deployment scenarios | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | For 6GR paging transmission/reception, study at least the following aspects:<br>- Study paging transmission scheme(s) to facilitate network energy savings<br>- Study paging scheme(s) to facilitate UE energy savings<br>- Study necessity of paging capacity enhancement<br>- Study necessity of paging coverage enhancement | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | Study 6GR signals, channels and procedures for initial access and idle mobility, considering at least<br>- Cell/Initial Cell search and cell ID identification<br>- Time/frequency synchronization/tracking<br>- Beam measurement<br>- System information acquisition<br>- Whether TRP is transparent/non-transparent to UE during above procedures<br>Study 6GR signals, channels and procedures for idle mobility, considering at least<br>- Cell search and cell ID identification<br>- Time/frequency synchronization/tracking<br>- Beam measurement<br>- System information acquisition<br>- Whether TRP is transparent/non-transparent to UE during above procedures | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | For 6GR measurements in initial access and for mobility, study measurement resource, measurement quantity, measurement functionality and measurement procedure, at least including:<br>- L1 measurements<br>- Cell-level/[TRP-level] and beam-level measurement | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | Study beam operation during 6GR initial access, including:<br>- Beam acquisition during initial access<br>- Beam report/refinement during initial access<br>- Feasibility and performance of spatial/temporal beam prediction during initial access | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | Basic 6GR sync signal structure is defined as 6GR SSB, which consists of primary synchronization signal(s), secondary synchronization signal(s) and physical broadcast channel(s)<br>- FFS: Other types of 6GR sync signal/channel structure or reference signal and their structure | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | Study random access framework with the following aspects:<br>- Enablement of energy efficient random access procedures (supporting SID objective 1b);<br>  - Including both network and UE power saving<br>- Coverage improvement (supporting SID objective 1d);<br>- Support of random access for diverse device types and capabilities (supporting SID objective 1g);<br>- System performance improvement from overhead reduction, simplification of signaling/configurations (supporting SID objective 1k);<br>- Additionally consider following aspects<br>  - random access latency;<br>  - capacity<br>  - detection reliability;<br>  - high speed mobility;<br>- Note: Other aspects identified during future discussions are not excluded.<br>The following scenarios and assumptions beyond single carrier/TRP are considered for the study of above random access framework:<br>- NTN<br>- SBFD<br>- multi-carrier<br>- multi-TRP<br>- Note: whether/how to support one or more of the scenarios/assumptions, including whether any special handling or functionality needs to be introduced in support of the scenarios/assumptions is part of the study. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |

| Agreement | For basic initial access procedures considering UEs with different bandwidth capabilities, study the determination of frequency location(s) and bandwidth(s) for UE to receive/monitor the downlink signals/channels during initial access and idle mode for at least the following:<br>- System information<br>- Random access procedure<br>- Paging<br>- FFS: Relation to frequency location of 6G synchronization signals | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | For basic initial access procedures considering UEs with different bandwidth capabilities, study the determination of the frequency location(s) and bandwidth(s) for UE to transmit uplink signals/channels during initial access and idle mode:<br>- FFS: Relation to the DL frequency location(s) and bandwidth(s) in TDD<br>- FFS: Relation to frequency location of 6G synchronization signals | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| Agreement | - 6G PDCCH study will consider at least the following concepts:<br>      - CORESET<br>      - CCE<br>      - REG as the minimum resource unit<br>      - REG bundle<br>      - CCE to REG mapping<br>      - Search Space<br>      - PDCCH candidate and CCE aggregation levels<br>      - Blind decoding<br>      - DMRS for PDCCH<br>      - Hash function<br>- FFS the details of the concepts above<br>- FFS the relation among CCE, REG, REG bundle, and PDCCH candidates | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | For 6G PDCCH study, consider at least the following aspects:<br>  - Control-channel blocking<br>  - Coverage<br>  - UE energy consumption<br>  - UE complexity<br>  - Coexistence of different device types<br>  - Reliability of PDCCH transmission<br>  - NW complexity and scheduling<br>  - Resource efficiency and overhead<br>  - Latency<br>  - MRSS | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Working Assumption | - REG consists of multiple REs.<br>- CCE consists of a set of REGs.<br>- PDCCH candidate corresponds to one or multiple CCE(s).<br>- REG bundle consists of a set of REGs. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |

| Agreement | If 6GR supports to define uplink control information (UCI) in Layer 1, study how to define the method(s) to convey the UCI over physical channel, at least (but not limited to) the following aspects:<br>- Option 1: Define a UCI dedicated physical channel, i.e., Physical Uplink Control Channel (PUCCH)<br>- Option 2: UCI carried on PUSCH<br>- Option 3: Other method(s)<br>Note: Whether UCI is carried in L1 or L2 to be discussed in agenda item 10.5.3.1 and 10.5.4.3 | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | Support following table as the basic assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH transmission scheme.<br>Note: Additional EVM assumptions for AI/ML based evaluation can be further discussed.<br>\| Parameters \| Proposals \|<br>\| #1 Frequency range \| Around 0.7 GHz, FDD Around 2 GHz, FDD Around 4 GHz, TDD Around 7 GHz, TDD Around 30 GHz, TDD \|<br>\| #2 Multiple access \| OFDMA \|<br>\| #2-1 waveform \| CP-OFDM and DFTS-OFDM \|<br>\| #3 Numerology \| 15 kHz for FDD 30 kHz for TDD and around 2—7 GHz 120 kHz for TDD and around 30 GHz \|<br>\| #4 Scenario \| Suburban macro, 1732 m ISD (for around 0.7—4 GHz) Urban macro, 500 m ISD (for around 0.7—30 GHz) Dense urban, 200 m ISD (for around 4—30 GHz) Urban Macro (500m), one-layer deployment, two-layer deployment (Optional) Dense urban (200m), one-layer deployment, two-layer deployment (Optional) Urban Grid (Optional) Other scenarios are not precluded (companies to report) \|<br>\| #4-1 Deployment \| 7 x 3, single layer, hex grid (baseline) 7 x 3 two layers, macro layer: hex grid; micro layer: locations of micro sites are reported by company (optional) 19 x 3, single layer, hex grid (optional) \|<br>\| #5 Channel model \| TR 38.901 \|<br>\| #6 System bandwidth \| 20 MHz (baseline) 100 MHz (for 4—7 GHz) (optional) Other bandwidths are not precluded (companies to report) \|<br>\| #7 gNB RX antenna setup and port layouts (𝑀,𝑁,𝑃,𝑀𝑔,𝑁𝑔,𝑀𝑝,𝑁𝑝) \| Reuse SLS assumption for PDSCH (AI 10.5.2.2) and additional assumption as follows: Around 4 GHz: 4 TXRUs, 32 AEs, (M, N, P, Mg, Ng, Mp, Np) = (8, 2, 2, 1, 1; 1, 2), (dH, dV) = (0.5, 0.8) (optional) \|<br>\| #8 BS receiver noise figure \| Around 7 GHz and below: 5dB Around 15 GHz and above: 7dB \|<br>\| #9 BS receiver \| Baseline: MMSE-IRC Other can be reported \|<br>\| #10 BS scheduler \| Proportional fair \|<br>\| #11 Modulation \| Up to 256 QAM Other can be reported \|<br>\| #12 MIMO scheme \| Depending on PUSCH scheme Reported by company (e.g., SU/MU-MIMO, max rank) \|<br>\| #13 UE speed \| Urban macro and dense urban: indoor (3 km/h), outdoor (30km/h) Suburban macro: indoor (3 km/h), outdoor (40km/h) additional assumption of 0.3 km/h for FWA \|<br>\| #14 UE TX antenna configuration \| Details follow corresponding agreements in Agenda 10.1. Other antenna location/configuration are not precluded and can be considered. \|<br>\| FFS: CPE configuration \|  \|<br>\| #15 Traffic model (FFS) \|  \|<br>\| #16 Precoder granularity \| Company can report \|<br>\| #17 Backhaul assumption \| Ideal backhaul (baseline) Non-ideal backhaul (optional) \|<br>\| #18 Power control \| Company can report \|<br>\| #19 UE power class \| 23dBm, 26dBm[,29dBm] \|<br>\| #20 Metric \| Throughput Additional assumption of average UPT, 5%-tile UPT, 50%-tile UPT, 95%-tile UPT \|<br>\| #21 Impairments \| Company can report (according to use case / scenario / scheme) e.g., UE coherence modeling, Tx power imbalance, relative phase error, DL&UL reciprocity \|<br>- Note: EVM assumption for HST scenarios will be treated separately | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |

| Agreement | Study the adaptation of PDCCH monitoring in 6GR for UE energy efficiency purpose, considering at least:<br>- Impact on network energy efficiency, as well as complexity and performance<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>Note: 6GR DL WUS related discussions are handled in AI 10.6. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |

| Agreement | RAN1 to study L1 signalling framework for time-domain scheduling/resource allocation for downlink and uplink transmission by considering, e.g., within a slot or across the slot boundary, PXSCH repetitions, and other necessary aspects if any.<br>- Note1: PXSCH corresponds to PDSCH or PUSCH.<br>- Note2: it doesn’t mean those examples are confirmed<br>- Note3: The descriptions/figures for scheduling options in [R1-2601584, FL summary] is an example for information. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR, DL and UL HARQ operation designs considers at least the following aspects:<br>  - latency<br>  - reliability<br>  - coverage<br>  - power saving (NW and UE)<br>  - NW complexity<br>  - UE complexity<br>  - diverse services/applications/traffics<br>  - system efficiency/system throughput/user throughput<br>  - feedback efficiency/UL and DL overhead<br>Note: the design of DL and UL HARQ does not necessarily be the same | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | For DL HARQ in 6GR, study both following HARQ-ACK feedback mechanisms<br>- Mechanism 1: HARQ-ACK information bits are transmitted via L1 signalling<br>- Mechanism 2: HARQ-ACK information bits are transmitted via higher layer signalling (e.g., MAC CE) | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | For discussion purposes,<br>- Asynchronous HARQ refers to that retransmission(s) occurs in a non pre-determined occasion once the corresponding initial transmission is scheduled.<br>- Adaptive HARQ refers that the transmission parameters and resources for the retransmission can be adaptively adjusted.<br>- For DL and UL in 6GR, support asynchronous and adaptive HARQ operation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | Study possible HARQ-ACK payload size range. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR, support at least TB level granularity for HARQ-ACK feedback | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | Study the UE-to-UE cross-link interference for 6GR:<br>- Study and identify the applicable scenarios of UE-to-UE cross-link interference.<br>- Study the characteristics of UE-to-UE cross-link interference, for example what is the potential interference signal level, what is the potential impact on system operation.<br>- Study the mechanisms and techniques for handling UE-to-UE cross-link interference, including UE-to-UE cross-link interference measurement and reporting<br>  - For each candidate mechanism, evaluate and analyze the performance benefit, impact to the system and complexity at BS/UE.<br>  - Study the candidate resource or signals/channels for measuring UE-to-UE cross-link interference.<br>  - Study and identify the candidate measurement quantities.<br>  - Study the reporting mechanisms | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | Study BS-to-BS cross-link interference for 6GR:<br>- Study and identify the applicable scenarios of BS-to-BS cross-link interference.<br>- Study the characteristics of BS-to-BS cross-link interference, for example what is the potential interference signal level, what is the potential impact to the system operation.<br>- Study the mechanisms and techniques that can handle the BS-to-BS cross-link interference, including the mechanisms for measuring and identifying the BS-to-BS cross-link interference:<br>  - For each considered mechanism, evaluate the performance benefit, impact to the system operation, and complexity at BS/UE.<br>  - The candidate resource for measuring BS-to-BS cross-link interference.<br>  - The measurement quantities at least for evaluation purpose. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | For handling remote interference in 6GR:<br>- Study the applicable scenarios for remote interference between remote cells due to atmospheric ducting;<br>- Study the impact of remote interference to the system, including the impact to the uplink reception.<br>- Study the characteristics of the remote interference.<br>- Study the candidate mechanisms for measuring/detecting/identifying remote interference, mechanisms to mitigate remote interference.<br>  - For each candidate mechanism, evaluate the benefits and impact to the system.<br>Note: UE reporting to request uplink resource scheduling is to be discussed under this agenda | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | Study the UE reporting mechanism for requesting uplink resource with considering at least the following aspects:<br>- Applicable use cases, at least including requesting resource for UL data transmission<br>- UL transmission latency<br>- Signalling overhead<br>- UL resource efficiency<br>- System capacity<br>- Network/UE complexity<br>- other aspects are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | Targeting for same coverage as 6G PDCCH in the same band, Study DL WUS coverage by considering at least the following aspects.<br>- Missed detection rate<br>- False alarm rate<br>- RRC state differences<br>- Different use cases<br>- Reference configuration of PDCCH | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| Agreement | The same SCS is assumed for DL WUS as for the 6GR Sync signals in the same band if sync signals and data channels use the same SCS.<br>FFS SCS of DL WUS if sync signals and data channels use different SCS in FR2-1 | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| Agreement | Study the following aspects of DL WUS design:<br>- Time or frequency domain sequence definition<br>- Time and frequency resource allocation<br>- Multiplexing and coexistence with other signals and channels, including DL WUS<br>- PAPR and BS/UE processing complexity<br>- Network overhead and NW&UE energy efficiency<br>- Other aspects are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| Agreement | Study 6GR DL WUS triggering PDCCH monitoring with and without C-DRX in RRC connected state, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>- Impact to UPT, if applicable<br>- Impact to latency, if applicable<br>- Impact to QoS/delay budget satisfaction rate, if applicable<br>- Network overhead/complexity<br>- Network energy consumption<br>- Other impacts, if any | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | For RRC idle state, study serving cell RRM measurement based on 6GR measurement signal (e.g., at least 6GR sync signal) by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>  - measurement based on EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring vs measurement based on non-EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring<br>- coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>For RRC idle state, study neighboring cell RRM measurement based on 6GR measurement signal (e.g., at least 6GR sync signal) by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>  - measurement based on EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring vs measurement based on non-EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring<br>- Neighboring cell identification, measurement and evaluation<br>- Neighboring cell number limitation, if any<br>- Inter-cell interference<br>- Coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>- FFS the power consumption of neighboring cell RRM measurement based on EE processing<br>Note: It doesn’t mean the measurement in EE processing has to be coupled with DL WUS monitoring | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | Study further necessity and feasibility of UL WUS under the following deployment scenario (DS):<br>- DS#1: Standalone cell where UE obtains UL WUS configuration e.g., from standalone cell and/or pre-defined in the specifications<br>  - DS#1a: Prior to UL WUS, nothing transmitted on the standalone cell<br>  - DS#1b: Prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with or without PBCH on the standalone cell but no SIB1 on the standalone cell<br>  - DS#1c: Prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on the standalone cell and periodic SIB1 on the standalone cell<br>  - Note: UL WUS is transmitted to the standalone cell<br>- RAN1 to consider at least<br>  - Coverage target of UL WUS<br>  - How UE acquires synchronization for UL WUS<br>  - How UE decides UL WUS transmission power<br>  - RRC states for the above scenarios<br>- Note: The above scenarios can be applicable to multi-TRP deployment scenario if TRP is transparent to UE at the time of UL WUS transmission<br>- Note: The above scenarios are for study purposes only and do not imply 6GR support. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | Study further necessity and feasibility of UL WUS under the following deployment scenario (DS):<br>- DS#2: Multi-cell/carrier where UE obtains UL WUS configuration from cell/carrier #1<br>  - Always-on 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on cell/carrier #1, and SIB1 on cell/carrier #1<br>  - DS#2a: Prior to UL WUS, nothing transmitted on cell/carrier #2<br>  - DS#2b: Prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with or without PBCH on cell/carrier #2 but no SIB1 on cell/carrier #2<br>  - DS#2c: Prior to UL WUS, 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on cell/carrier #2 and periodic SIB1 on cell/carrier #2<br>  - Note: UL WUS can be transmitted to cell/carrier #1 or cell/carrier #2<br>- RAN1 to consider at least<br>  - Coverage target of UL WUS<br>  - How UE acquires synchronization for UL WUS<br>  - How UE decides UL WUS transmission power<br>  - Whether the cells/carriers are in the same band or not<br>  - Whether the cells/carriers are collocated or not<br>  - RRC states for the above scenarios<br>  - Whether the above scenarios can be applicable to multi-TRP deployment scenario<br>- Note: The above scenarios are for study purposes only and do not imply 6GR support. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | For NTN link budget template, RAN1 to take the TN link budget template as baseline with specific rows / values (including adding new rows) to be further discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601469.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | RAN1 will define evaluation parameters for at least the following combinations of satellite orbit and bands:<br>- S-band:<br>  - LEO 300, LEO 600, GEO<br>- Ka band:<br>  - [LEO 300], LEO 600, [LEO 1200], GEO<br>- Ku band:<br>  - LEO 1200, GEO<br>NOTE 1: The evaluations for S band are expected to be similar to L-band.<br>NOTE 2: This is only for the purpose of evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601469.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | RAN1 to use the following terminology when discussing GNSS availability at least for physical layer operation.<br>- GNSS-based: Refers to the network mode of operation which relies on devices being equipped with a GNSS receiver and the devices can obtain a position fix within a given accuracy<br>  - FFS: How often the UE may be required to obtain a position fix, which may be related to the required accuracy.<br>  - FFS: if position under this operation can be obtained by means other than GNSS that provides a comparable accuracy, e.g. pre-configuration for a fixed device. This may also include information other than positioning.<br>- GNSS-degraded: Refers to the network mode of operation which relies on devices being equipped with a GNSS receiver, the devices were able to obtain a position fix at some point in time, but the devices may not currently have a position fix within a given accuracy.<br>  - NOTE: The UE may be able to use the position fix for physical layer operation<br>- GNSS-free/GNSS-less: Refers to the network mode of operation which does not rely on devices being equipped with a GNSS receiver, or devices are equipped with a GNSS receiver but do not have a current position fix that can be used for physical layer operation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601469.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | 6GR NTN targets to support GNSS-based operation, GNSS-degraded operation and GNSS-less/GNSS-free operation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601470](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601470.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | 6GR NTN uplink time-frequency synchronization follows the same principle as NR NTN as baseline:<br>- The concept of “uplink synchronization reference point” is introduced in 6GR NTN.<br>- 6GR NTN provides satellite assistance information<br>- At least for GNSS-based operation, it is supported that UE uses its own location information + satellite assistance information to perform time-frequency pre-compensation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601470](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601470.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Conclusion | As a general principle for 6GR NTN study:<br>- Under NTN agenda item, we will identify issues / requirements specific to NTN.<br>- Potential solutions to these issues / requirements may be studied under the NTN agenda<br>  - The outcome of this study may be discussed under other agenda items if common design is possible.<br>  - These solutions may end up resulting in an extension of the TN design.<br>  - This may depend on the solution / issue / requirement<br>- NTN specific solutions may be introduced when a common / extended design cannot meet the NTN requirements.<br>- When targeting a common design TN performance is prioritized. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601470](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601470.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | 6GR supports large scheduling offsets to accommodate the RTT introduced by the satellite channel. Further discuss how to realize these scheduling offsets:<br>- Option 1: Reuse the k_offset concept from NR as a baseline with potential modifications.<br>  - NOTE: Under this option, additional scheduling offsets (e.g. similar to K1/K2 in NR) may be supported for scheduling flexibility, which apply in addition to k_offset.<br>- Option 2: The large scheduling delays to accommodate the RTT are incorporated into the scheduling offsets (e.g. similar to K1/K2 in NR), which may or may not be common for TN and NTN | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601471](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601471.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Conclusion | When reporting inputs for the link budget template and evaluation assumptions to RAN1#124b, companies are encouraged to provide them in an xls attached to their contribution following the format of the xls attached to R1-2601471. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601471](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601471.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | Study NTN specific issues/requirements at least for the following aspects for 6GR NTN under this agenda:<br>- Uplink time-frequency synchronization<br>- HARQ issues: e.g. enable/disable HARQ feedback (which may include how to efficiently operate with HARQ feedback enabled/disabled), number of HARQ processes, etc.<br>- Timing relationships with large RTT.<br>- Coverage target<br>- Physical layer aspects of multi-satellite operation (including multi-orbit)<br>- Aspects related to Multiple beams per satellite<br>- Aspects related to satellite having more beam footprints than simultaneously active beams<br>Other aspects are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601471](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601471.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
